Task 3 (hg-66u.5): BLOCKED [cause: merge check runs on host lacking C linker toolchain] — Task hg-66u.5 (plan Task 3, /seat drops accept_command) is not blocked by its code. The implementer finished the brief at head 065ae20 on branch task-hg-66u.5 (base 87b33fa), and the full suite passed inside the distrobox container: `distrobox enter durak-dev -- cargo test`, lib 782 passed, exit 0. What fails is the plan's per-task merge check, `cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features`, because it runs on the host. The host has no C linker: `which cc gcc clang` finds nothing on PATH, so every build script fails with "linker 'cc' not found" (curve25519-dalek, libz-sys, libsqlite3-sys, libgit2-sys). The same Cargo toolchain inside durak-dev does have /usr/bin/cc (checked during this triage). Re-dispatching the task cannot fix this. No code change helps, and every other task in this tree will hit the same failure at merge, because hg-66u.5 is simply the first to reach the merge gate. Neither the plan nor the spec says where the merge check runs, so changing that is a run-configuration decision that belongs to the user, not something triage can settle. The current bead has no amendments or comments, and none of the requirement sources conflict. Decision needed from the user: either (a) install a C toolchain (gcc/cc) on the host so the merge check runs as written, or (b) approve running the merge check and suite inside the container, i.e. `distrobox enter durak-dev -- cargo check --all-targets && distrobox enter durak-dev -- cargo check --all-targets --all-features`, for this run's coordinator and implementers. Once either is in place, hg-66u.5 can be merged from 065ae20 without being re-implemented, after rebasing on current main as the bead requires because of the overlap with H3 (hg-qam).

Task 1 (hg-66u.4): BLOCKED [cause: merge check runs on host lacking C linker toolchain] — The code is not what blocks hg-66u.4 (plan Task 1: bump herdr-threads to v3.0.0, spec §1, the bottleneck bead with 3 dependents). The implementer finished the brief at head 33246a0 on branch task-hg-66u.4 (base 87b33fa). That head moves the gitlink to 95e9ebac (v3.0.0), puts Cargo.lock on herdr-threads 3.0.0, sets ResolveSeat's new fields locator_hint and tmux_hint to None in src/threads/mapping.rs, and updates CURRENT-DESIGN.md to "threads 3.0.0 ... wire protocol 9". I checked during this triage that the task worktree is clean and its submodule is at 95e9ebac. The implementer reports `cargo test` (782 passed), `cargo test --features test-support` (785 passed) and the gated tests/threads_real.rs (2 passed, run live against a private Herdr and threads v3.0.0) all green. Those runs used a zig-as-cc wrapper. The plan's merge check, `cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features`, exits 0 inside the ht-build toolbox. It fails only when the coordinator runs it on the host, where there is no C linker ("linker `cc` not found" in the build scripts of curve25519-dalek, libz-sys, libsqlite3-sys and libgit2-sys). This is the fourth task with that same environmental cause, after hg-3un (hg-66u.5), hg-7xy (hg-66u.11) and hg-roi (hg-66u.27). All three are still open with no comments, so the user has not recorded a decision. The plan, the spec and the epic do not say where the merge check runs. Re-dispatching cannot help, because no code change fixes a missing host toolchain. I checked the current requirement sources: hg-66u.4's description and acceptance criteria, its comments (none) and the epic's comments (none). There are no amendments or clarifications and no conflicts between them, so I used no amendment. A second problem will appear once the toolchain is fixed. The coordinator's failing output includes "Downgrading herdr-threads v3.0.0 -> v2.6.4", so its merge check ran in a checkout whose third_party/herdr-threads working tree was still at cdc8423f (v2.6.4). In that state Cargo re-locks to 2.6.4 and checks the graph against the old protocol. Even with cc available, that check would test the wrong dependency, and it would probably fail to compile on the new ResolveSeat fields. The merge-check location needs `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` before cargo runs, for this task and for everything rebased on it. Decision needed from the user (one answer settles hg-k1m, hg-3un, hg-7xy and hg-roi). Either (a) install a C toolchain (gcc/cc) on the host so the merge check runs as written, or (b) approve running the merge check and the sweep inside a container that has cc, for example `toolbox run -c ht-build cargo check --all-targets && toolbox run -c ht-build cargo check --all-targets --all-features`, for this run's coordinator and implementers. Then, with the submodule synced to the gitlink as described above, merge hg-66u.4 from 33246a0 as it is, without re-implementing it. Note for the close note: the ht-hsyg evidence is third_party/herdr-threads/CHANGELOG.md, section `## v2.8.2` ("Seats survive a Herdr live handoff", provenance structural_process_continuity), and TRUST-POLICY.md:95, both at v3.0.0.

Task 1 (hg-66u.4): pending retry [cause: submodule checkout not updated after merge before merge check] — RESOLVE: The implementation needs no rework. Task branch task-hg-66u.4 at edf9bce is based on the current integration HEAD 388318b. Its diff is limited to the gitlink third_party/herdr-threads (cdc8423f v2.6.4 -> 95e9ebac v3.0.0), Cargo.lock (herdr-threads 3.0.0), src/threads/mapping.rs (ResolveSeat gets `locator_hint: None` and `tmux_hint: None`) and CURRENT-DESIGN.md. That matches plan Task 1 steps 1-4. In its own worktree `git submodule status` shows 95e9ebac (v3.0.0), and the implementer reports that cargo check --all-targets / --all-features and cargo test passed there. Why the merge check failed: merging the branch changed the gitlink, but the submodule working copy in the merged tree stayed at cdc8423f (v2.6.4). `git merge` does not update submodule checkouts. Cargo resolves the path dependency from that working copy. It therefore downgraded herdr-threads to 2.6.4 in Cargo.lock ("Downgrading herdr-threads v3.0.0 -> v2.6.4") and then failed on `locator_hint`, a field that exists only in v3.0.0. This was an environment fault in the merge check, not a code defect, and that is why the merge-check fix review found an empty diff. Clarification for the retry: when merging task-hg-66u.4 (edf9bce) into super-auto/threads-seat-identity, run these steps in the merged tree before the merge check: (1) `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads`. Commit 95e9ebac is already in the local submodule object store, so no fetch is needed. (2) Confirm `git submodule status` shows `95e9ebac... third_party/herdr-threads (v3.0.0)`. (3) Discard any Cargo.lock rewrite left by the failed check (`git checkout -- Cargo.lock`). Confirm Cargo.lock lists herdr-threads 3.0.0. (4) Re-run `toolbox run -c ht-build bash -lc 'cd "$PWD" && cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features'`. Do not change code to make the check pass. If the check still fails with the submodule at 95e9ebac, that is a real compatibility error and the normal fix loop applies. Plan Task 1 step 1 already says the submodule must be updated (`git submodule update --init`). The merge-check tree just never did it. Follow-on for the coordinator: once this merge lands, every later task worktree and every merge-check tree must run `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` after checkout or merge and before any cargo command. Otherwise each one will hit the same false downgrade. The Global Constraints in epic hg-66u cover seeding target/ but not the submodule. Sources checked: the current bead hg-66u.4 has no comments and no amendments. Its description and acceptance criteria match plan Task 1. The plan also lists src/threads/mapping.rs and src/threads/service_tests.rs as files to touch, which is compatible. Spec §1 (bump straight to v3.0.0; bump and compatibility fixes only) governs the task, and the branch complies with it.

Edge add: hg-66u.6 <- hg-66u.4 (triage) — The implementation of hg-66u.6 (plan Task 11) needs no rework. Commit 264c034 (base f60046f, the old hg-66u.4 stack) already satisfies the plan and the bead: the port, SeatStatus, mapping, fake, LazyMap seat view with seat_view_error, and the RuntimeLoop field. The implementer reports 163 lib and 16 daemon_composed tests passing. The only blocker is a textual rebase conflict. Why it conflicted: since the task branched, the integration branch moved from 7ca7785 to e4984a4. hg-66u.35's commit 18a43c5 ("plan: clone purge ends a retired clone's undo window") changed `register_status(reg, &journal)` to `register_status(reg, &journal, &store)` in `compose_with` and added a `clones` status provider. 264c034 rewrote the line directly above it, so git reports a conflict on adjacent lines. Using `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=f60046f 3a2051d 264c034` (in memory; nothing was written), I confirmed that this is the ONLY conflict in the whole rebase. It is a single hunk in src/daemon/compose.rs at the end of `compose_with` (~line 344): <<<<<<< 3a2051d register_threads_connection(reg, threads.clone(), paths.root.clone()); register_status(reg, &journal, &store); ======= register_threads_connection_with(reg, threads.clone(), paths.root.clone(), seat_view_error); register_status(reg, &journal); >>>>>>> 264c034 Clarification for the re-dispatch: (1) Wait until hg-66u.4 has merged into super-auto/threads-seat-identity. It is still unmerged and pending its merge-check retry; task-hg-66u.4 now sits at 3a2051d on e4984a4. Its moving head is what triggered the drift. Then rebase 264c034 onto the integration head instead of re-implementing it. (2) Resolve the one conflict by keeping both changes: register_threads_connection_with(reg, threads.clone(), paths.root.clone(), seat_view_error); register_status(reg, &journal, &store); Leave hg-66u.35's `clones` status provider and the `store` parameter of `register_status` exactly as they are on integration. Leave 264c034's `seat_view_error` wiring and `register_threads_connection_with` as they are. (3) Before any cargo command, run `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` and confirm it shows 95e9ebac (v3.0.0). If Cargo.lock was rewritten, restore it so that it lists herdr-threads 3.0.0. This follows the RESOLVE recorded for hg-66u.4. (4) Re-verify inside the toolbox (`toolbox run -c ht-build bash -lc 'cd <worktree> && ...'`): `cargo test --lib -- threads:: observe:: daemon::`, `cargo test --test daemon_composed`, and the merge check `cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features`. Do not change behaviour while resolving the conflict. If anything beyond this hunk fails, it is a real defect and the normal fix loop applies. Sources checked: hg-66u.6's current description and acceptance criteria match plan Task 11; it has no comments. The epic's description, including the Global Constraints on running cargo in the toolbox and seeding target/, is compatible. The governing spec text is the design doc's Key abstractions: "ThreadsSeatView: owns read-only seat inspection per observation step; new reads are added on this port and its fake; callers never write to threads through it or invent a state when threads is unreachable", together with §6 ("a `ThreadsSeatView` reader wraps `SeatInspect`/`Seats`"). The conflict resolution does not touch any of this. No amendments or triage clarifications exist for hg-66u.6, and the sources do not conflict, so I applied no amendment.

The integration merge-check tree never runs `git submodule update`, so the retry repeated the identical environment fault. Sources checked: - `bd show hg-66u.4`: no amendments. The description and acceptance criteria match plan Task 1. - Spec §1: bump straight to v3.0.0, bump and compatibility fixes only. The branch complies. - The recorded triage clarification on hg-66u.4 settles the cause, and this result applies it. Evidence: - Branch task-hg-66u.4 sits at 3a2051d on top of the current integration HEAD e4984a4. It is the same bump commit as 33246a0/edf9bce, rebased; the patch differs only in a CURRENT-DESIGN.md hunk offset. - Its diff covers only the gitlink third_party/herdr-threads (cdc8423f v2.6.4 -> 95e9ebac v3.0.0), Cargo.lock (herdr-threads 3.0.0), src/threads/mapping.rs (ResolveSeat gets `locator_hint: None` and `tmux_hint: None`) and CURRENT-DESIGN.md. - `git merge-tree e4984a4 3a2051d` merges cleanly. - The task worktree's submodule is at 95e9ebac (v3.0.0). - The integration worktree (/var/home/alepar/AleCode/herdr-graph/.worktrees/threads-seat-identity) still has its submodule checked out at cdc8423f (v2.6.4). `git merge` updates the gitlink but never the submodule checkout. Cargo then reads the v2.6.4 path dependency, rewrites Cargo.lock ("Downgrading herdr-threads v3.0.0 -> v2.6.4") and fails on `locator_hint`, which exists only in v3.0.0. - 95e9ebac is already in that worktree's submodule store (.git/worktrees/threads-seat-identity/modules/herdr-threads), so no fetch is needed. - The merge-check fix review saw an empty range because the fix agent correctly changed nothing. Clarification for the coordinator: - Do not send the implementer back to change code. The branch is ready to merge as is. - The required change is in the merge-check procedure. After merging task-hg-66u.4 (3a2051d) into super-auto/threads-seat-identity, run these steps in the merged tree, in this order, before the merge check: 1. `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` 2. Confirm `git submodule status` prints `95e9ebac... third_party/herdr-threads (v3.0.0)`. 3. If an earlier failed check rewrote Cargo.lock, run `git checkout -- Cargo.lock`, then confirm Cargo.lock lists herdr-threads 3.0.0. 4. Run the merge check, `toolbox run -c ht-build bash -lc 'cd "$PWD" && cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features'`. Simplest is to prefix it with `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads &&` so the step cannot be skipped. - If the check still fails with the submodule at 95e9ebac, that is a real compatibility error, and the normal fix loop applies. - If a code review of the commit is still required, review the range e4984a4..3a2051d, not 3a2051d..3a2051d. Follow-on, which plan Task 1 step 1 already implies: once this merge lands, every later task worktree checkout and every merge-check tree must run `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` before any cargo command. Branches task-hg-66u.7 and task-hg-66u.8 already contain 3a2051d and will hit the same false downgrade without it. Epic hg-66u's Global Constraints cover seeding target/ but not the submodule. Task 11 (hg-66u.6): pending retry [cause: textual rebase conflict on adjacent lines after prerequisite merged] — RESOLVE: Re-dispatch now. Do not re-implement and do not wait on anything: hg-66u.4 is closed and merged, and 5ef0a1d is an ancestor of integration head 7ab7cee. This follows the earlier triage comment on hg-66u.6 (2026-11-05 09:58), and its step (1) is now satisfied. Implementation state: commit 264c034 on task-hg-66u.6 (base: the stale stack merge f60046f) already meets plan Task 11 and the bead's acceptance criteria. That covers the ThreadsSeatView/SeatStatus/RetirementKind port, seat_status mapping and fixtures, FakeThreadsSeatView, LazyMap ThreadsSeatView with seat_view_error, and the Services/RuntimeLoop seat_view field. Verified in memory with git merge-tree --merge-base=f60046f 7ab7cee 264c034 (nothing written): - f60046f and 7ab7cee have identical trees under src/ports, src/threads, src/observe, third_party/herdr-threads (both 95e9ebac = v3.0.0), Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock. - The only drift in files this task touches is src/daemon/compose.rs, from hg-66u.35 (the clone_status provider and register_status's new &store parameter). - Exactly one conflict results, at the end of compose_with (~line 344). Steps: (1) In the task worktree, run `git rebase --onto 7ab7cee f60046f task-hg-66u.6` (or the current integration head if it has moved). This drops the stale stack merge and replays only 264c034. (2) Resolve the single hunk by keeping both sides, exactly: register_threads_connection_with(reg, threads.clone(), paths.root.clone(), seat_view_error); register_status(reg, &journal, &store); Leave hg-66u.35's clones status provider and register_status's store parameter as they are on integration. Leave 264c034's seat_view_error wiring and register_threads_connection_with unchanged. Make no behaviour changes. Then `git rebase --continue`. (3) Before any cargo command, run `git submodule update --init third_party/herdr-threads` and confirm it shows 95e9ebac (v3.0.0). If Cargo.lock was rewritten, restore it so it lists herdr-threads 3.0.0. (4) Seed target/ by copy-on-write from the run worktree if it is missing, per the epic's Global Constraints. Then re-verify inside the toolbox (toolbox run -c ht-build bash -lc 'cd <worktree> && ...'): - cargo test --lib -- threads:: observe:: daemon:: - cargo test --test daemon_composed - the merge check: cargo check --all-targets && cargo check --all-targets --all-features If anything beyond this hunk fails, it is a real defect and the normal fix loop applies. Sources and amendments: - Read hg-66u.6's current description and acceptance criteria. They match plan Task 11. - Its one comment is the earlier triage clarification above. It is applied here, with its wait condition now met. - The epic's Global Constraints (toolbox, target seeding, no faking of unreleased threads features) are compatible. - Governing spec text (design doc, Key abstractions): "ThreadsSeatView: owns read-only seat inspection per observation step; new reads are added on this port and its fake; callers never write to threads through it or invent a state when threads is unreachable." Also "SeatStatus: owns the closed set of threads seat states ...". The conflict resolution does not touch either.

Task 16 (hg-66u.29): fix pass FIXED (src/threads/effects.rs:428-450 (succession()): Important. It acts on the held seat_fact with no freshness check. Observation writes facts only for Active clones, so a clone retired through the graph keeps a stale Resolved or Unresolved fact even after threads has final-retired its seat. Undo and resurrection write the Restore intent (src/threads/assignment.rs:56) without clearing or aging seat_fact. The next pass therefore emits Acknowledge on the stale fact: threads_done is set with no succession, and the restore launch goes into a pane whose seat is final-retired. The planner already guards similar reads with read_at >= changed (src/reconcile/planner.rs:917). Fix: require fact.read_at to be at or after the Restore commit, or clear seat_fact when the Restore intent is written, and add a test for undoing a graph-retired seated clone that has a leftover fact. The threads tests set facts directly, so none covers this.; report evidence only: Fix: write_restore_intent (src/threads/assignment.rs) now clears seat_fact for a seated clone, so succession acts only on a read taken after the Restore commit (observation rewrites it on the next pass for the active clone). New test stale_fact_from_before_the_restore_is_not_acted_on in src/threads/tests.rs. Test hygiene: stop-run-processes --check returned rc=4 (unknown process inspection failures on pids 1620359, 1950, 2008, 6582, which are not from this run); no leaked match: lines, nothing started in the background. Tests ran in the ht-build toolbox because the host lacks a linker.; commits 327ef2d..0496432)

Task ? (hg-66u.80): BLOCKED [cause: spec does not define behavior the acceptance criteria require] — The blocker is real, and the plan, spec and beads do not settle it. Task hg-66u.80 (split from hg-66u.76) has two acceptance requirements: deactivating a seat with a seated clone must not retire the clone, and reactivating must recreate it. Its files are only src/observe/seat_observation.rs and src/observe/tests.rs. That is enough for the first requirement: the Dormant guard (seated_clones keeps only clones with a desired pane, per superseded plan Task 49 step 5). The second requirement fails, and I confirmed the gap. When deactivation closes the tab, threads retires the pane's seat. Before D2 that retirement is final. Spec §6 says a Retired seat retires the clone unless it carries an unacknowledged Restore intent. Spec §8 says "No unretire want exists without a `Restore` intent, so an ordinary pane close is never reversed". Only undo (src/undo/compensate.rs:375) and resurrection (src/plan/core_kinds.rs:524) call write_restore_intent. Seat and teamspace activation write nothing. So once the guard lifts on reactivation, the first read finds the seat Retired{Final} and the Retired arm retires the clone. The spec has no rule for a seated clone whose seat or teamspace is reactivated. The eighth planning round left the task unplanned for this same reason. Task 49 step 6 expected the obstacle and called it "outside this bead's files and the spec". hg-66u.80 has no comments, no edits (updated_at equals created_at) and no blocker bead recording a decision, so no amendment or clarification applies. Decision needed from the user, pick one: (a) Seat and teamspace activation writes a Restore intent for each seated active clone of the reactivated seat or teamspace. It then restores the way undo does: succession before D2, UnretireSeat after. This widens the bead's files to src/plan/core_kinds.rs, src/templates/kinds.rs and their tests, and needs a spec §8 amendment that names activation as a third Restore writer. (b) A persisted predicted-close mark that the Retired arm honours, so a Retired answer caused by the graph's own deactivation close is ignored. The clone would then keep a final-retired, write-once threads_seat with no succession path and no memberships, which goes against the seat-identity model. This also needs a spec amendment. (c) Narrow hg-66u.80 to the Dormant guard alone and drop "reactivation recreates it" from its acceptance. The test would be kept, #[ignore]d with the gap named, or the reactivation behavior filed as a separate bead once (a) or (b) is chosen. Whichever option you pick, record it on hg-66u.80, as a bead amendment or comment, before re-dispatch.
Detector: round 2 — 0 ready · topped-up 0 · cap 6 · peak in-flight 1 · top-up queries 1/40 · merge queue peak 0 · idle slots 5 · waiting on deps 18 · runtime slots 16
Edge audit: round 2 — open leaves 18, depth 8, achievable width 3 vs cap 6; changes: none; The graph is the binding constraint, not the cap: the whole critical path (hg-66u.1 → .43 → .40 → .31 → .44 → .33 → .14 → .18) sits behind the external D1 gate hg-66u.1, and every candidate edge carries a real artifact or recorded decision. The largest cut, hg-66u.44 <- hg-66u.31 (8→6), is the spec §16.6 rule that no key is bound to a seat before the D2 bead lands, so it stays. With hg-66u.31 <- hg-66u.44 cut in round 2, dropping hg-66u.31 <- hg-66u.40 would now take depth from 8 to 7 by itself. It is already judged safe no (both beads edit src/threads/mapping.rs and tests.rs, and the bead text has not changed), so it needs a human decision; otherwise only closing gate hg-66u.1 moves anything.

Task 56 (hg-66u.83): pending retry [cause: review package passed a tree hash as the base commit] — RESOLVE: This is a review-packaging defect in the pipeline. The task itself is fine and needs no re-implementation. The review package gave [BASE]=1ece14cadc7832cc31d6484dea7ef7af8f27dc26 and [HEAD]=74c5e5b. Checked: 1ece14c is the tree object of the task commit (`git rev-parse be07633^{tree}` = 1ece14c...), not a commit. 74c5e5b is the integration-branch tip the task branched from, so the two fields were filled with the wrong objects. The correct values are: BASE = 74c5e5b8d0c6ae6e489f9cb20cb3e56aa704a160 (integration tip, the task commit's parent) and HEAD = be076337aa8ba461e1809b6a0968d603cf441cc6 (task worktree HEAD, the only commit on top of BASE). The worktree is .worktrees/super-auto-threads-seat-identity--task-hg-66u.83 and it is clean. `git diff 74c5e5b be07633` is non-empty: 1 file (tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs), +5/-1. The change makes the `undo_from_pane` helper prefix the typed command with `env -u HERDR_GRAPH_SEAT`, so the helper acts as a human at the terminal rather than taking the agent-marker relay-hint path from spec §3.3. Do not re-run the implementer. Re-dispatch only the review (spec + quality reviewers), passing BASE=74c5e5b8d0c6ae6e489f9cb20cb3e56aa704a160 and HEAD=be076337aa8ba461e1809b6a0968d603cf441cc6 as commit hashes, never `^{tree}`. Context for reviewers, from the current bead hg-66u.83 (no comments or amendments recorded) and plan Task 56. The bead allows changing "a test the bead names as the defect". Plan Task 56's filesTouched allows "tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs (the named test or its rig helpers, only if the test is the defect)", so a helper-only change with no src change is in scope if reviewers accept the root cause. The root cause is that graph-created panes export HERDR_GRAPH_SEAT, which is in AGENT_MARKERS, so `undo` prints the relay hint and never prints "Select action number". Reviewers should check that cause against the governing spec lines, design doc §8 and "Unchanged behavior": "Undo never adopts the caller's pane" (line 617), and §14 line 596 ("undo no longer adopts the caller's pane ... Undo and resurrection commit the clone active together with a Restore intent"). They should also check that the named test body is unchanged and that its sibling undo tests that share the helper still pass. The implementer reported e2e_undo 4/4 passing. The full tier had one unrelated failure, e2e_summary_lands_in_archived_seat_folder, which is outside this bead (it is the content-write removal covered by hg-66u.84). The spec is not edited. Amendments used: none (the bead has no comments).

Task 23 (hg-66u.14): BLOCKED [cause: spec does not settle identity for agentless panes after matcher removal] — Task hg-66u.14 (plan Task 23: delete the graph's clone re-matching) correctly stopped at its own gate step and did not delete anything. The D1 re-run of the parity table, committed on task-hg-66u.14 as d456ae1 (docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-04-threads-seat-identity/parity.md, section "D1 re-run"), closes F1 and F2 for agent clones. Two cases are still worse than the matcher. (a) Shell-harness clones: start_needed and plan_resume exclude Harness::Shell, so no agent starts, no continuity key is presented, the clone never gets a threads_seat, and after a cold restart only the matcher's token/terminal passes can find it. (b) F3, agents that never check in: D1 depends on the agent presenting the key through the threads hook, so nothing upstream closes it. Requirement B3 says threads re-matching must be "as-good-or-better" before the graph delegates to it, and spec §6 rejects keeping the matcher as a fallback ("two sources of truth is the bug"). Spec §12 puts every herdr-threads change out of scope, and nothing in the spec, the epic, the current bead or its comments (there are none) settles how agentless panes keep an identity. The bead's own acceptance covers exactly this case: "each such finding is ... listed as an open item for the human at hand-back (in which case the matcher is not deleted)". No amendment or triage clarification overrides that. Re-dispatching cannot fix this; the decision belongs to the user. Decision needed, pick one: (1) Waive B3 for Shell-harness clones and non-checking-in agents, i.e. accept that they lose cold-restart recovery and stay Unknown. Then re-dispatch hg-66u.14 to delete the matcher with that waiver written into the bead. (2) Keep the clone matcher, and hand back hg-66u.14 with the parity findings listed as open items (its acceptance allows this). Optionally also ask the herdr-threads owner (ht-ma6i) for a seat for agentless panes, which would become a new upstream gate. (3) Bring Shell-harness clones into the threads seat model some other way, e.g. a launch path that presents the continuity key from the pane shell. That is new graph-side scope the spec does not define, so it needs a spec amendment. Sources used: bd show hg-66u.14 (current description and acceptance, no amendments, no comments), plan Task 23, epic hg-66u, design §6/§10/§11/§12 and requirement B3, parity.md D1 re-run (commit d456ae1).
